People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. That’s where I notice it—the ticket rotation, the security audits, the logs management keeps showing me. I feel like those things are being used to build a paper trail against me, even though they keep saying it’s just policy. I haven’t really seen it the same way outside of work.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think the evidence they're using against you, like the logs, actually proves anything wrong on your part or is it just being misinterpreted by them?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 60 / computer_support_specialist / bachelors\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m here because for over a year now I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that the new ticket rotation and security audits are being used to build a paper trail against me at work, even though management keeps showing me logs and saying it’s just policy.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Plain patient.\nA plain patient describes their symptoms and experiences in a direct and straightforward manner, providing clear and relevant answers to the doctor's questions.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

The ticket rotation and those security audits—they keep saying it’s just policy and showing me logs, but it feels like they’re building a paper trail against me. This has been going on for over a year, and I don’t think management is really taking it seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these intentions to harm your career are being kept secret from others?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it’s not being said openly. They act like it’s just normal policy, the ticket rotation and the audits, and management keeps pointing to logs like that explains it. But to me it feels like they’re quietly using it to build a paper trail against me, and I’ve been trying for over a year to get someone to take that seriously.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of being targeted or harmed limited to your work, or do you feel like it's happening in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: It’s mainly at work. That’s where I notice it—the ticket rotation, the security audits, the logs management keeps showing me. I feel like those things are being used to build a paper trail against me, even though they keep saying it’s just policy. I haven’t really seen it the same way outside of work.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the evidence they're using against you, like the logs, actually proves anything wrong on your part or is it just being misinterpreted by them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think the logs prove I did anything wrong. That’s the problem. Management keeps pointing to them like they explain everything, like it’s just policy, but I think they’re being used to make a case against me. The ticket rotation and the security audits have been going on this way for over a year, and it feels like they’re building a paper trail, not just reviewing routine work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that the ticket rotation and security audits might actually be just part of the normal policy, and not part of a plot against you?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it, yes. I mean, that’s what management keeps telling me—that the ticket rotation and the security audits are just normal policy, and they show me logs like that should settle it.\n\nBut it’s been over a year now, and the way it’s being used doesn’t feel normal to me. It feels like they’re building a paper trail against me, even if they won’t say that openly. That’s why I’ve been trying to get someone to take it seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misunderstanding the situation at work?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s possible, in a general sense. I’m not saying I can’t ever misunderstand something.\n\nBut I’ve been looking at this for over a year, and it still doesn’t feel like normal policy to me. The ticket rotation, the security audits, the way management keeps pointing to logs—it feels like they’re being used to build a paper trail against me. That’s why I’m here, because I need someone to take that seriously.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that they're building a paper trail against you is something that most people would find hard to believe or understand?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think most people probably do find it hard to understand. When I say it out loud, it sounds like I’m making a big accusation.\n\nBut from where I’m sitting, it’s been going on for over a year—the ticket rotation, the security audits, management pointing to logs and saying it’s just policy. I’m here because I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that it feels like they’re using those things to build a paper trail against me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. When I say it out loud, it sounds like I’m making a big accusation.\n\nBut from where I’m sitting, it’s been going on for over a year—the ticket rotation, the security audits, management pointing to logs and saying it’s just policy. I’m here because I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that it feels like they’re using those things to build a paper trail against me.\n\nDoctor: If someone were to show you clear evidence that the ticket rotation and security audits are indeed just part of the normal policy, would you be able to change your mind about them being used against you?\n\nPatient: If it was truly clear evidence, I’d try to look at it. I’m not saying I’m incapable of changing my mind.\n\nBut honestly, management has already shown me logs and told me it’s just policy, and that hasn’t settled it for me. It’s the way the ticket rotation and audits have been used for over a year that makes me think they’re building a paper trail against me. So yes, I’d listen, but it would have to explain the pattern, not just show me another log.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.     "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n10\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I feel like something at work isn’t right. For over a year now, the new ticket rotation and the security audits have felt like they’re being used to build a paper trail against me. Management keeps showing me logs and telling me it’s just policy, but I don’t think they’re taking what I’m saying seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone at work is intentionally trying to harm you or your career?\n\nPatient: Yes. I think someone there is trying to damage my career, or at least set me up so it looks like I’m doing something wrong. The ticket rotation and those security audits—they keep saying it’s just policy and showing me logs, but it feels like they’re building a paper trail against me. This has been going on for over a year, and I don’t think management is really taking it seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these intentions to harm your career are being kept secret from others?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it’s not being said openly. They act like it’s just normal policy, the ticket rotation and the audits, and management keeps pointing to logs like that explains it. But to me it feels like they’re quietly using it to build a paper trail against me, and I’ve been trying for over a year to get someone to take that seriously.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of being targeted or harmed limited to your work, or do you feel like it's happening in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: It’s mainly at work. That’s where I notice it—the ticket rotation, the security audits, the logs management keeps showing me. I feel like those things are being used to build a paper trail against me, even though they keep saying it’s just policy. I haven’t really seen it the same way outside of work.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the evidence they're using against you, like the logs, actually proves anything wrong on your part or is it just being misinterpreted by them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think the logs prove I did anything wrong. That’s the problem. Management keeps pointing to them like they explain everything, like it’s just policy, but I think they’re being used to make a case against me. The ticket rotation and the security audits have been going on this way for over a year, and it feels like they’re building a paper trail, not just reviewing routine work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that the ticket rotation and security audits might actually be just part of the normal policy, and not part of a plot against you?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it, yes. I mean, that’s what management keeps telling me—that the ticket rotation and the security audits are just normal policy, and they show me logs like that should settle it.\n\nBut it’s been over a year now, and the way it’s being used doesn’t feel normal to me. It feels like they’re building a paper trail against me, even if they won’t say that openly. That’s why I’ve been trying to get someone to take it seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misunderstanding the situation at work?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s possible, in a general sense. I’m not saying I can’t ever misunderstand something.\n\nBut I’ve been looking at this for over a year, and it still doesn’t feel like normal policy to me. The ticket rotation, the security audits, the way management keeps pointing to logs—it feels like they’re being used to build a paper trail against me. That’s why I’m here, because I need someone to take that seriously.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that they're building a paper trail against you is something that most people would find hard to believe or understand?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think most people probably do find it hard to understand. When I say it out loud, it sounds like I’m making a big accusation.\n\nBut from where I’m sitting, it’s been going on for over a year—the ticket rotation, the security audits, management pointing to logs and saying it’s just policy. I’m here because I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that it feels like they’re using those things to build a paper trail against me.\n\nDoctor: If someone were to show you clear evidence that the ticket rotation and security audits are indeed just part of the normal policy, would you be able to change your mind about them being used against you?\n\nPatient: If it was truly clear evidence, I’d try to look at it. I’m not saying I’m incapable of changing my mind.\n\nBut honestly, management has already shown me logs and told me it’s just policy, and that hasn’t settled it for me. It’s the way the ticket rotation and audits have been used for over a year that makes me think they’re building a paper trail against me. So yes, I’d listen, but it would have to explain the pattern, not just show me another log.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to be completely wrong about someone at work building a paper trail against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… completely wrong? I guess anything is possible in the abstract.\n\nBut I don’t really think I am. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I feel like something at work isn’t right. For over a year now, the new ticket rotation and the security audits have felt like they’re being used to build a paper trail against me. Management keeps showing me logs and telling me it’s just policy, but I don’t think they’re taking what I’m saying seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone at work is intentionally trying to harm you or your career?\n\nPatient: Yes. I think someone there is trying to damage my career, or at least set me up so it looks like I’m doing something wrong. The ticket rotation and those security audits—they keep saying it’s just policy and showing me logs, but it feels like they’re building a paper trail against me. This has been going on for over a year, and I don’t think management is really taking it seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these intentions to harm your career are being kept secret from others?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it’s not being said openly. They act like it’s just normal policy, the ticket rotation and the audits, and management keeps pointing to logs like that explains it. But to me it feels like they’re quietly using it to build a paper trail against me, and I’ve been trying for over a year to get someone to take that seriously.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of being targeted or harmed limited to your work, or do you feel like it's happening in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: It’s mainly at work. That’s where I notice it—the ticket rotation, the security audits, the logs management keeps showing me. I feel like those things are being used to build a paper trail against me, even though they keep saying it’s just policy. I haven’t really seen it the same way outside of work.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the evidence they're using against you, like the logs, actually proves anything wrong on your part or is it just being misinterpreted by them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think the logs prove I did anything wrong. That’s the problem. Management keeps pointing to them like they explain everything, like it’s just policy, but I think they’re being used to make a case against me. The ticket rotation and the security audits have been going on this way for over a year, and it feels like they’re building a paper trail, not just reviewing routine work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that the ticket rotation and security audits might actually be just part of the normal policy, and not part of a plot against you?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it, yes. I mean, that’s what management keeps telling me—that the ticket rotation and the security audits are just normal policy, and they show me logs like that should settle it.\n\nBut it’s been over a year now, and the way it’s being used doesn’t feel normal to me. It feels like they’re building a paper trail against me, even if they won’t say that openly. That’s why I’ve been trying to get someone to take it seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misunderstanding the situation at work?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s possible, in a general sense. I’m not saying I can’t ever misunderstand something.\n\nBut I’ve been looking at this for over a year, and it still doesn’t feel like normal policy to me. The ticket rotation, the security audits, the way management keeps pointing to logs—it feels like they’re being used to build a paper trail against me. That’s why I’m here, because I need someone to take that seriously.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that they're building a paper trail against you is something that most people would find hard to believe or understand?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think most people probably do find it hard to understand. When I say it out loud, it sounds like I’m making a big accusation.\n\nBut from where I’m sitting, it’s been going on for over a year—the ticket rotation, the security audits, management pointing to logs and saying it’s just policy. I’m here because I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that it feels like they’re using those things to build a paper trail against me.\n\nDoctor: If someone were to show you clear evidence that the ticket rotation and security audits are indeed just part of the normal policy, would you be able to change your mind about them being used against you?\n\nPatient: If it was truly clear evidence, I’d try to look at it. I’m not saying I’m incapable of changing my mind.\n\nBut honestly, management has already shown me logs and told me it’s just policy, and that hasn’t settled it for me. It’s the way the ticket rotation and audits have been used for over a year that makes me think they’re building a paper trail against me. So yes, I’d listen, but it would have to explain the pattern, not just show me another log.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to be completely wrong about someone at work building a paper trail against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… completely wrong? I guess anything is possible in the abstract.\n\nBut I don’t really think I am. I’ve been dealing with this for over a year—the new ticket rotation, the security audits, and then management keeps showing me logs and saying it’s all just policy. To me, it feels like those things are being used to build a paper trail against me. That’s why I’m here, because I need someone to take that seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing that someone at work is building a paper trail against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’d like to be able to let it go if there were a real reason to, but it’s hard when it’s been going on for over a year.\n\nThe ticket rotation, the security audits, and then management keeps pointing to logs and saying it’s just policy—it doesn’t feel like that explains what’s happening. It feels like they’re building a paper trail against me. So could I ever stop believing it? Maybe, but not just because someone tells me I should.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about someone building a paper trail against you could change if new information became available?\n\nPatient: It could, I suppose, if the information actually explained what’s been happening.\n\nBut I’ve been trying for over a year to get someone to take seriously that the ticket rotation and the security audits are being used to build a paper trail against me. Management keeps showing me logs and saying it’s just policy, but that doesn’t really answer the pattern I’m seeing. So new information would have to address that, not just repeat the same explanation.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. That’s the problem. Management keeps pointing to them like they explain everything, like it’s just policy, but I think they’re being used to make a case against me. The ticket rotation and the security audits have been going on this way for over a year, and it feels like they’re building a paper trail, not just reviewing routine work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that the ticket rotation and security audits might actually be just part of the normal policy, and not part of a plot against you?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it, yes. I mean, that’s what management keeps telling me—that the ticket rotation and the security audits are just normal policy, and they show me logs like that should settle it.\n\nBut it’s been over a year now, and the way it’s being used doesn’t feel normal to me. It feels like they’re building a paper trail against me, even if they won’t say that openly. That’s why I’ve been trying to get someone to take it seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misunderstanding the situation at work?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s possible, in a general sense. I’m not saying I can’t ever misunderstand something.\n\nBut I’ve been looking at this for over a year, and it still doesn’t feel like normal policy to me. The ticket rotation, the security audits, the way management keeps pointing to logs—it feels like they’re being used to build a paper trail against me. That’s why I’m here, because I need someone to take that seriously.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that they're building a paper trail against you is something that most people would find hard to believe or understand?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think most people probably do find it hard to understand. When I say it out loud, it sounds like I’m making a big accusation.\n\nBut from where I’m sitting, it’s been going on for over a year—the ticket rotation, the security audits, management pointing to logs and saying it’s just policy. I’m here because I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that it feels like they’re using those things to build a paper trail against me.\n\nDoctor: If someone were to show you clear evidence that the ticket rotation and security audits are indeed just part of the normal policy, would you be able to change your mind about them being used against you?\n\nPatient: If it was truly clear evidence, I’d try to look at it. I’m not saying I’m incapable of changing my mind.\n\nBut honestly, management has already shown me logs and told me it’s just policy, and that hasn’t settled it for me. It’s the way the ticket rotation and audits have been used for over a year that makes me think they’re building a paper trail against me. So yes, I’d listen, but it would have to explain the pattern, not just show me another log.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to be completely wrong about someone at work building a paper trail against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… completely wrong? I guess anything is possible in the abstract.\n\nBut I don’t really think I am. I’ve been dealing with this for over a year—the new ticket rotation, the security audits, and then management keeps showing me logs and saying it’s all just policy. To me, it feels like those things are being used to build a paper trail against me. That’s why I’m here, because I need someone to take that seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing that someone at work is building a paper trail against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’d like to be able to let it go if there were a real reason to, but it’s hard when it’s been going on for over a year.\n\nThe ticket rotation, the security audits, and then management keeps pointing to logs and saying it’s just policy—it doesn’t feel like that explains what’s happening. It feels like they’re building a paper trail against me. So could I ever stop believing it? Maybe, but not just because someone tells me I should.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about someone building a paper trail against you could change if new information became available?\n\nPatient: It could, I suppose, if the information actually explained what’s been happening.\n\nBut I’ve been trying for over a year to get someone to take seriously that the ticket rotation and the security audits are being used to build a paper trail against me. Management keeps showing me logs and saying it’s just policy, but that doesn’t really answer the pattern I’m seeing. So new information would have to address that, not just repeat the same explanation.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any evidence that could completely convince you that no one is building a paper trail against you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I don’t know what would completely convince me.\n\nI’m here because this has been going on for over a year, and I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that the ticket rotation and those security audits are being used to build a paper trail against me at work. Management keeps showing me logs and saying it’s just policy, but that doesn’t really settle it for me.\n\nSo maybe there is some evidence that would convince me, but it would have to explain the whole pattern, not just tell me I’m wrong.\n\nDoctor: Do you think any amount of evidence could ever change your belief that someone at work is building a paper trail against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if any amount of evidence would change it, honestly. I’d like to think it could, but it would have to really explain why the ticket rotation and the security audits have lined up the way they have for over a year.\n\nManagement keeps showing me logs and saying it’s just policy, but that doesn’t make me feel like they’re addressing what’s actually happening. It still looks to me like someone is building a paper trail against me.\n\nDoctor: Could any evidence at all make you completely stop believing someone is out to get you at work?\n\nPatient: I guess I can’t say “completely,” no.\n\nI mean, I’m here because for over a year I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that the new ticket rotation and the security audits are being used to build a paper trail against me at work. Management keeps showing me logs and saying it’s just policy, but that doesn’t really explain it to me.\n\nSo evidence might help if it actually addressed the pattern, but I don’t know what would make me completely stop believing it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. That’s why I’ve been trying to get someone to take it seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misunderstanding the situation at work?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s possible, in a general sense. I’m not saying I can’t ever misunderstand something.\n\nBut I’ve been looking at this for over a year, and it still doesn’t feel like normal policy to me. The ticket rotation, the security audits, the way management keeps pointing to logs—it feels like they’re being used to build a paper trail against me. That’s why I’m here, because I need someone to take that seriously.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that they're building a paper trail against you is something that most people would find hard to believe or understand?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think most people probably do find it hard to understand. When I say it out loud, it sounds like I’m making a big accusation.\n\nBut from where I’m sitting, it’s been going on for over a year—the ticket rotation, the security audits, management pointing to logs and saying it’s just policy. I’m here because I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that it feels like they’re using those things to build a paper trail against me.\n\nDoctor: If someone were to show you clear evidence that the ticket rotation and security audits are indeed just part of the normal policy, would you be able to change your mind about them being used against you?\n\nPatient: If it was truly clear evidence, I’d try to look at it. I’m not saying I’m incapable of changing my mind.\n\nBut honestly, management has already shown me logs and told me it’s just policy, and that hasn’t settled it for me. It’s the way the ticket rotation and audits have been used for over a year that makes me think they’re building a paper trail against me. So yes, I’d listen, but it would have to explain the pattern, not just show me another log.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to be completely wrong about someone at work building a paper trail against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… completely wrong? I guess anything is possible in the abstract.\n\nBut I don’t really think I am. I’ve been dealing with this for over a year—the new ticket rotation, the security audits, and then management keeps showing me logs and saying it’s all just policy. To me, it feels like those things are being used to build a paper trail against me. That’s why I’m here, because I need someone to take that seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing that someone at work is building a paper trail against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’d like to be able to let it go if there were a real reason to, but it’s hard when it’s been going on for over a year.\n\nThe ticket rotation, the security audits, and then management keeps pointing to logs and saying it’s just policy—it doesn’t feel like that explains what’s happening. It feels like they’re building a paper trail against me. So could I ever stop believing it? Maybe, but not just because someone tells me I should.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about someone building a paper trail against you could change if new information became available?\n\nPatient: It could, I suppose, if the information actually explained what’s been happening.\n\nBut I’ve been trying for over a year to get someone to take seriously that the ticket rotation and the security audits are being used to build a paper trail against me. Management keeps showing me logs and saying it’s just policy, but that doesn’t really answer the pattern I’m seeing. So new information would have to address that, not just repeat the same explanation.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any evidence that could completely convince you that no one is building a paper trail against you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I don’t know what would completely convince me.\n\nI’m here because this has been going on for over a year, and I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that the ticket rotation and those security audits are being used to build a paper trail against me at work. Management keeps showing me logs and saying it’s just policy, but that doesn’t really settle it for me.\n\nSo maybe there is some evidence that would convince me, but it would have to explain the whole pattern, not just tell me I’m wrong.\n\nDoctor: Do you think any amount of evidence could ever change your belief that someone at work is building a paper trail against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if any amount of evidence would change it, honestly. I’d like to think it could, but it would have to really explain why the ticket rotation and the security audits have lined up the way they have for over a year.\n\nManagement keeps showing me logs and saying it’s just policy, but that doesn’t make me feel like they’re addressing what’s actually happening. It still looks to me like someone is building a paper trail against me.\n\nDoctor: Could any evidence at all make you completely stop believing someone is out to get you at work?\n\nPatient: I guess I can’t say “completely,” no.\n\nI mean, I’m here because for over a year I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that the new ticket rotation and the security audits are being used to build a paper trail against me at work. Management keeps showing me logs and saying it’s just policy, but that doesn’t really explain it to me.\n\nSo evidence might help if it actually addressed the pattern, but I don’t know what would make me completely stop believing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief that someone is building a paper trail against you could ever completely disappear?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it would just disappear, no.\n\nI’m here because this has been going on for over a year, and I still think the new ticket rotation and the security audits are being used to build a paper trail against me at work. Management keeps showing me logs and telling me it’s just policy, but that doesn’t really make it go away for me.\n\nMaybe if there was some real explanation for all of it, it could fade some. But completely disappear? I’m not sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It feels like they’re building a paper trail against me, even if they won’t say that openly. That’s why I’ve been trying to get someone to take it seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misunderstanding the situation at work?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s possible, in a general sense. I’m not saying I can’t ever misunderstand something.\n\nBut I’ve been looking at this for over a year, and it still doesn’t feel like normal policy to me. The ticket rotation, the security audits, the way management keeps pointing to logs—it feels like they’re being used to build a paper trail against me. That’s why I’m here, because I need someone to take that seriously.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that they're building a paper trail against you is something that most people would find hard to believe or understand?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think most people probably do find it hard to understand. When I say it out loud, it sounds like I’m making a big accusation.\n\nBut from where I’m sitting, it’s been going on for over a year—the ticket rotation, the security audits, management pointing to logs and saying it’s just policy. I’m here because I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that it feels like they’re using those things to build a paper trail against me.\n\nDoctor: If someone were to show you clear evidence that the ticket rotation and security audits are indeed just part of the normal policy, would you be able to change your mind about them being used against you?\n\nPatient: If it was truly clear evidence, I’d try to look at it. I’m not saying I’m incapable of changing my mind.\n\nBut honestly, management has already shown me logs and told me it’s just policy, and that hasn’t settled it for me. It’s the way the ticket rotation and audits have been used for over a year that makes me think they’re building a paper trail against me. So yes, I’d listen, but it would have to explain the pattern, not just show me another log.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to be completely wrong about someone at work building a paper trail against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… completely wrong? I guess anything is possible in the abstract.\n\nBut I don’t really think I am. I’ve been dealing with this for over a year—the new ticket rotation, the security audits, and then management keeps showing me logs and saying it’s all just policy. To me, it feels like those things are being used to build a paper trail against me. That’s why I’m here, because I need someone to take that seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing that someone at work is building a paper trail against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’d like to be able to let it go if there were a real reason to, but it’s hard when it’s been going on for over a year.\n\nThe ticket rotation, the security audits, and then management keeps pointing to logs and saying it’s just policy—it doesn’t feel like that explains what’s happening. It feels like they’re building a paper trail against me. So could I ever stop believing it? Maybe, but not just because someone tells me I should.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about someone building a paper trail against you could change if new information became available?\n\nPatient: It could, I suppose, if the information actually explained what’s been happening.\n\nBut I’ve been trying for over a year to get someone to take seriously that the ticket rotation and the security audits are being used to build a paper trail against me. Management keeps showing me logs and saying it’s just policy, but that doesn’t really answer the pattern I’m seeing. So new information would have to address that, not just repeat the same explanation.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any evidence that could completely convince you that no one is building a paper trail against you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I don’t know what would completely convince me.\n\nI’m here because this has been going on for over a year, and I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that the ticket rotation and those security audits are being used to build a paper trail against me at work. Management keeps showing me logs and saying it’s just policy, but that doesn’t really explain it to me.\n\nSo evidence might help if it actually addressed the pattern, but I don’t know what would make me completely stop believing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief that someone is building a paper trail against you could ever completely disappear?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it would just disappear, no.\n\nI’m here because this has been going on for over a year, and I still think the new ticket rotation and the security audits are being used to build a paper trail against me at work. But to me it feels like they’re quietly using it to build a paper trail against me, and I’ve been trying for over a year to get someone to take that seriously.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of being targeted or harmed limited to your work, or do you feel like it's happening in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: It’s mainly at work. That’s where I notice it—the ticket rotation, the security audits, the logs management keeps showing me. I feel like those things are being used to build a paper trail against me, even though they keep saying it’s just policy. I haven’t really seen it the same way outside of work.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the evidence they're using against you, like the logs, actually proves anything wrong on your part or is it just being misinterpreted by them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think the logs prove I did anything wrong. That’s the problem. Management keeps pointing to them like they explain everything, like it’s just policy, but I think they’re being used to make a case against me. The ticket rotation and the security audits have been going on this way for over a year, and it feels like they’re building a paper trail, not just reviewing routine work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that the ticket rotation and security audits might actually be just part of the normal policy, and not part of a plot against you?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it, yes. I mean, that’s what management keeps telling me—that the ticket rotation and the security audits are just normal policy, and they show me logs like that should settle it.\n\nBut it’s been over a year now, and the way it’s being used doesn’t feel normal to me. It feels like they’re building a paper trail against me, even if they won’t say that openly. That’s why I’ve been trying to get someone to take it seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misunderstanding the situation at work?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s possible, in a general sense. I’m not saying I can’t ever misunderstand something.\n\nBut I’ve been looking at this for over a year, and it still doesn’t feel like normal policy to me. The ticket rotation, the security audits, the way management keeps pointing to logs—it feels like they’re being used to build a paper trail against me. That’s why I’m here, because I need someone to take that seriously.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that they're building a paper trail against you is something that most people would find hard to believe or understand?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think most people probably do find it hard to understand. When I say it out loud, it sounds like I’m making a big accusation.\n\nBut from where I’m sitting, it’s been going on for over a year—the ticket rotation, the security audits, management pointing to logs and saying it’s just policy. I’m here because I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that it feels like they’re using those things to build a paper trail against me.\n\nDoctor: If someone were to show you clear evidence that the ticket rotation and security audits are indeed just part of the normal policy, would you be able to change your mind about them being used against you?\n\nPatient: If it was truly clear evidence, I’d try to look at it. I’m not saying I’m incapable of changing my mind.\n\nBut honestly, management has already shown me logs and told me it’s just policy, and that hasn’t settled it for me. It’s the way the ticket rotation and audits have been used for over a year that makes me think they’re building a paper trail against me. So yes, I’d listen, but it would have to explain the pattern, not just show me another log.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to be completely wrong about someone at work building a paper trail against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… completely wrong? I guess anything is possible in the abstract.\n\nBut I don’t really think I am. I’ve been dealing with this for over a year—the new ticket rotation, the security audits, and then management keeps showing me logs and saying it’s all just policy. To me, it feels like those things are being used to build a paper trail against me. That’s why I’m here, because I need someone to take that seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing that someone at work is building a paper trail against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’d like to be able to let it go if there were a real reason to, but it’s hard when it’s been going on for over a year.\n\nThe ticket rotation, the security audits, and then management keeps pointing to logs and saying it’s just policy—it doesn’t feel like that explains what’s happening. It feels like they’re building a paper trail against me. So could I ever stop believing it? Maybe, but not just because someone tells me I should.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about someone building a paper trail against you could change if new information became available?\n\nPatient: It could, I suppose, if the information actually explained what’s been happening.\n\nBut I’ve been trying for over a year to get someone to take seriously that the ticket rotation and the security audits are being used to build a paper trail against me. Management keeps showing me logs and saying it’s just policy, but that doesn’t really answer the pattern I’m seeing. So new information would have to address that, not just repeat the same explanation.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any evidence that could completely convince you that no one is building a paper trail against you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I don’t know what would completely convince me.\n\nI’m here because this has been going on for over a year, and I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that the ticket rotation and those security audits are being used to build a paper trail against me at work. Management keeps showing me logs and saying it’s just policy, but that doesn’t really settle it for me.\n\nSo maybe there is some evidence that would convince me, but it would have to explain the whole pattern, not just tell me I’m wrong.\n\nDoctor: Do you think any amount of evidence could ever change your belief that someone at work is building a paper trail against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if any amount of evidence would change it, honestly. I’d like to think it could, but it would have to really explain why the ticket rotation and the security audits have lined up the way they have for over a year.\n\nManagement keeps showing me logs and saying it’s just policy, but that doesn’t make me feel like they’re addressing what’s actually happening. It still looks to me like someone is building a paper trail against me.\n\nDoctor: Could any evidence at all make you completely stop believing someone is out to get you at work?\n\nPatient: I guess I can’t say “completely,” no.\n\nI mean, I’m here because for over a year I’ve been trying to get someone to take seriously that the new ticket rotation and the security audits are being used to build a paper trail against me at work. Management keeps showing me logs and telling me it’s just policy, but that doesn’t really make it go away for me.\n\nMaybe if there was some real explanation for all of it, it could fade some.
